# Operating System (OS): past exam questions

Every question from the photographed sheets, books and PDFs in `ex-question/` that belongs to this subject. The same question asked in several exams, or printed in several sources, is shown once. For each question you get **where it was asked** (the exams, with names normalised in `../exam-tags.json`) and **where it was taken from** (each source document, linked to its transcription and image).

- **Distinct questions:** 396 (from 478 records: Written 397, MCQ 71, Study note 8, Viva 2)
- **Exams it appears in:** 134
- **Corrections to printed answers:** 23
- **How counts work:** "Asked in (N)" counts different government/public-sector recruitment exams (BCS, NTRCA, BPSC, state banks, public bodies) and BUET M.Sc. admission tests. Private-bank exams and compiled viva experiences are listed but not counted. Coaching suggestion sheets and model tests never add to the count.

## Coaching "standard pattern" topics (not counted)

Topics the Bit to Exabyte question book lists as its standard pattern for this subject (from `../Standard Pattern/`). They show what the coaching expects, not what was asked.

- 04. RAID levels and how capacity is calculated — [Standard Pattern/01_super-ict-index-p1-q01-14.md](../Standard%20Pattern/01_super-ict-index-p1-q01-14.md)
- 07. Deadlock, four necessary conditions, prevention vs avoidance — [Standard Pattern/01_super-ict-index-p1-q01-14.md](../Standard%20Pattern/01_super-ict-index-p1-q01-14.md)
- 08. Banker's algorithm: safe state and safe sequence — [Standard Pattern/01_super-ict-index-p1-q01-14.md](../Standard%20Pattern/01_super-ict-index-p1-q01-14.md)
- 15. Process vs program; process state diagram — [Standard Pattern/02_super-ict-index-p2-q15-38.md](../Standard%20Pattern/02_super-ict-index-p2-q15-38.md)
- 25. Process vs program and process state diagram (repeat of 15) — [Standard Pattern/02_super-ict-index-p2-q15-38.md](../Standard%20Pattern/02_super-ict-index-p2-q15-38.md)
- 56. Linux commands: touch, mv, mkdir, cd, grep, chmod, ps, top, kill, ping and more — [Standard Pattern/03_super-ict-index-p3-q39-63.md](../Standard%20Pattern/03_super-ict-index-p3-q39-63.md)
- 63. Page vs frame; paging and fragmentation; page and frame bit calculation — [Standard Pattern/03_super-ict-index-p3-q39-63.md](../Standard%20Pattern/03_super-ict-index-p3-q39-63.md)
- 64. Virtual memory; page fault sequence — [Standard Pattern/04_super-ict-index-p4-q64-85.md](../Standard%20Pattern/04_super-ict-index-p4-q64-85.md)
- 74. Memory management with 8 GB RAM, 24 GB virtual memory, 14 GB demand — [Standard Pattern/04_super-ict-index-p4-q64-85.md](../Standard%20Pattern/04_super-ict-index-p4-q64-85.md)
- 77. Preemptive vs non-preemptive priority scheduling — [Standard Pattern/04_super-ict-index-p4-q64-85.md](../Standard%20Pattern/04_super-ict-index-p4-q64-85.md)
- 78. Short notes: process vs thread, paging vs segmentation, virtual vs physical memory, deadlock vs starvation, multiprogramming vs multitasking, context switching, kernel — [Standard Pattern/04_super-ict-index-p4-q64-85.md](../Standard%20Pattern/04_super-ict-index-p4-q64-85.md)
- 79. CPU scheduling: FCFS, SJF, preemptive priority with Gantt charts — [Standard Pattern/04_super-ict-index-p4-q64-85.md](../Standard%20Pattern/04_super-ict-index-p4-q64-85.md)
- 84. Logical and physical address bits (512 pages, 128 frames, 2 KB page) — [Standard Pattern/04_super-ict-index-p4-q64-85.md](../Standard%20Pattern/04_super-ict-index-p4-q64-85.md)
- 120. Linux file permissions and chmod — [Standard Pattern/06_super-ict-index-p6-q110-120-descriptive.md](../Standard%20Pattern/06_super-ict-index-p6-q110-120-descriptive.md)

<a id="g0151"></a>

### Write a Linux command to display the total number of lines of all .c and .h files in the … <sub>G0151</sub>

*Written*

Write a Linux command to display the total number of lines of all .c and .h files in the current directory.

- **Asked in (1):** BPDB AE(CSE) 2023
- **Source:**
  - BPDB Assistant Engineer (CSE) (BUET) (2023-02-24), Q3 — [BUET JOB/21_linux-count-and-max-subarray.md](../BUET%20JOB/21_linux-count-and-max-subarray.md) · [image](../BUET%20JOB/21_linux-count-and-max-subarray.jpg); [BUET MSC/02_bpdb-assistant-engineer-questions-1-9.md](../BUET%20MSC/02_bpdb-assistant-engineer-questions-1-9.md) · [image](../BUET%20MSC/02_bpdb-assistant-engineer-questions-1-9.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** ls -l *.c *.h | wc -l
- **Correction:** That counts files, not lines. Correct: cat *.c *.h | wc -l (or wc -l *.c *.h, whose last line is the total).

<a id="g4505"></a>

### Which of the following Linux command has incorrect syntax? (a) cat sampie.txt grep-v a| s… <sub>G4505</sub>

*MCQ*

Which of the following Linux command has incorrect syntax? (a) cat sampie.txt grep-v a| sort -r (b) Chown :group3 File1 (c) Chmod a+rx viewer.sh (d) None of the above

- **Asked in (1):** Combined Bank SO(IT) 2021
- **Source:**
  - Question paper found on the web (see the folder README for the URL) — Banker Selection Committee — Combined 2 Bank — Senior Officer (IT) — MCQ — 25.09.2021 — Exam taker: AUST (as printed by Stack IT; unverified) — 59 IT MCQs, MCQ49 — [bank-written-web/2021-combined2-so-it-mcq-aust-stackit-slideshare/combined2-so-it-2021-mcq.md](../bank-written-web/2021-combined2-so-it-mcq-aust-stackit-slideshare/combined2-so-it-2021-mcq.md)
- **Correction:** key b, but `chown :group3 File1` is valid (it changes only the group). The broken command is (a): `grep-v` lacks a space and the pipe from cat is missing (should be `cat sample.txt | grep -v a | sort -r`).

<a id="g4507"></a>

### What is the maximum size of a file allowed in Linux with the following data Block Size=4K… <sub>G4507</sub>

*MCQ*

What is the maximum size of a file allowed in Linux with the following data Block Size=4KB, inode data pointer size = 4 byte (a) 1 TB (b) Less than 4TB (c) 2TB+2GB+2MB+64KB (d) More than 4 TB

- **Asked in (1):** Combined Bank SO(IT) 2021
- **Source:**
  - Question paper found on the web (see the folder README for the URL) — Banker Selection Committee — Combined 2 Bank — Senior Officer (IT) — MCQ — 25.09.2021 — Exam taker: AUST (as printed by Stack IT; unverified) — 59 IT MCQs, MCQ51 — [bank-written-web/2021-combined2-so-it-mcq-aust-stackit-slideshare/combined2-so-it-2021-mcq.md](../bank-written-web/2021-combined2-so-it-mcq-aust-stackit-slideshare/combined2-so-it-2021-mcq.md)
- **Correction:** no working printed. With 12 direct, one single, one double and one triple indirect pointer: 12x4 KB + 1024x4 KB + 1024^2x4 KB + 1024^3x4 KB = 48 KB + 4 MB + 4 GB + 4 TB, just over 4 TB, option (d). (Real ext2/ext3 caps a file at 2 TB with 4 KB blocks because of the 32-bit sector count, which matches none of the options either.)

<a id="g0105"></a>

### A system has 4 processes and 3 resources; to avoid deadlock, the maximum number of proces… <sub>G0105</sub>

*MCQ*

A system has 4 processes and 3 resources; to avoid deadlock, the maximum number of processes that can execute simultaneously is: (3 / 2 / 1 / 4)

- **Asked in (1):** 49th BCS ICT (2025)
- **Source:**
  - 49th BCS written exam 2025 — ICT (281), question paper 2 (100 MCQ) (2025), Q59 — [BCS/58_euler-corollary-deadlock-insertion-sort.md](../BCS/58_euler-corollary-deadlock-insertion-sort.md) · [image](../BCS/58_euler-corollary-deadlock-insertion-sort.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** (ক) 3
- **Correction:** Question is underspecified (per-process resource demand not given) and the printed hold-and-wait reasoning is not a valid argument; in the usual version (3 instances, each process needs 2) the deadlock-free maximum is n(2−1)+1 ≤ 3 → 2. Verify against the original paper before using the key.

<a id="g0043"></a>

### What is deadlock? Describe the ways of detecting and protecting against deadlock. <sub>G0043</sub>

*Written*

What is deadlock? Describe the ways of detecting and protecting against deadlock.

- **Asked in (1):** 38th BCS (2018)
- **Source:**
  - 38th BCS Written (2018), Q7(c) — [BCS/29_circular-queue-solution-operating-system-deadlock.md](../BCS/29_circular-queue-solution-operating-system-deadlock.md) · [image](../BCS/29_circular-queue-solution-operating-system-deadlock.jpg); [BCS/24_deadlock-detection-recovery-os-hardware.md](../BCS/24_deadlock-detection-recovery-os-hardware.md) · [image](../BCS/24_deadlock-detection-recovery-os-hardware.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** Deadlock = processes each hold a resource and wait for another. Detection: a cycle in the resource-allocation graph (sufficient only when each resource has a single instance). Recovery: kill processes or preempt resources.
- **Correction:** Incomplete. It gives only 'hold and wait' of the four Coffman conditions and covers recovery instead of protection. Prevention means denying one of mutual exclusion, hold-and-wait, no preemption or circular wait; avoidance uses the Banker's algorithm.

### Banker's Algorithm: 5 processes P_0 through P_4; 3 resource types A (10 instances), B (5 … <sub>G0967</sub>

*Written*

Banker's Algorithm: 5 processes P_0 through P_4; 3 resource types A (10 instances), B (5 instances), and C (7 instances). - Snapshot at time $T_0$: | Process | Allocation (A B C) | Max (A B C) | Available (A B C) | |---|---|---|---| | P_0 | 0 1 0 | 7 5 3 | 3 3 2 | | P_1 | 2 0 0 | 3 2 2 | | | P_2 | 3 0 2 | 9 0 2 | | | P_3 | 2 1 1 | 2 2 2 | | | P_4 | 0 0 2 | 4 3 3 | | The content of the matrix. Need is defined to be Max – Allocation. | Process | Need (A B C) | |---|---| | P_0 | 7 4 3 | | P_1 | 1 2 2 |

## RAID

<a id="g0442"></a>

### How do you recover when a drive fails in a RAID array? <sub>G0442</sub>

*Written*

How do you recover when a drive fails in a RAID array?

- **Asked in (1):** Petrobangla AM(CSE) 2024
- **Source:**
  - BOGMC (Petrobangla) Assistant Manager (2024), Q5(b) — [PDF-TEXT/BOGMC_PetroBangla_Assistant_Manager_2024_Solutions_HUO_Academy.md](../PDF-TEXT/BOGMC_PetroBangla_Assistant_Manager_2024_Solutions_HUO_Academy.md#p6)
- **Printed answer:** Replace the failed drive and rebuild from mirror (RAID 1) or parity (RAID 5, RAID 6 tolerates 2 failures); RAID 0 has no redundancy, data lost.
